cat > "/home/uzu/Documents/grpc-llm-notes/proto-folder-and-llm-proto.md" << 'MDEOF'
Two things worth noticing:
is why the README calls the proto the "single source of truth".
stub wiring. You never hand-write any of that.
syntax = "proto3";
package llm;
option go_package = "llmgateway/gen/llm";
service LLMService {
gRPC shapes:
function call. Good for when you want the complete answer and can wait.
instead of making the client wait for the whole answer.
same idea from the other direction. This project deliberately keeps two to
stay learnable.)
Four messages describe all the data that crosses the wire:
message ChatRequest {
upstream HTTP API expects.
each piece of binary data belongs to. That's why protobuf is so compact (no
field names on the wire) and why it's **forbidden to reuse a number** after
one is removed.
Field numbers are also what make **schema evolution safe**: add a new field
mechanism is why teams trust protobuf contracts across dozens of services.
make proto
The same declaration maps to different native shapes per language:
message usage disagrees with the contract.
the contract lives in docs that age badly; here it lives in a file the
compiler enforces.
rpc Chat(ChatRequest) returns (ChatResponse);
rpc StreamChat(ChatRequest) returns (stream ChatChunk);
message Message {
string content = 2;
message ChatResponse {
string content = 1;
message ChatChunk {
schema evolution.
MDEOF
